\displaystyle\cup\{(e,w_{s})\mid e\in(\exists s)^{\mathcal{I}}\cap\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}},\mathcal{T}\models s\sqsubseteq r\}

\displaystyle\cup\{(w_{s},e)\mid e\in(\exists s)^{\mathcal{I}}\cap\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}},\mathcal{T}\models s\sqsubseteq r^{-}\}

Remark 5
.

Notice
\mathcal{I}_{0}
is a special case of the models
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{P}}
considered in Section
4.1
, and that
{\left|{\mathcal{I}_{0}}\right|}\leq{\left|{\mathcal{A}}\right|}+2{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}
.

The key result is now the following:

Lemma 25
.

Let
q
be an AQ.
If
\mathcal{I}
is a countermodel for
q
over
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
, then so is
\mathcal{I}_{0}
.

Proof.

Assume
\mathcal{I}
is a countermodel for
q
over
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Setting
\mathcal{P}=\{f_{r}\mid r\text{ is forced in }\mathcal{I}\}
, we have
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{P}}=\mathcal{I}_{0}
(see Section
4.1
) and thus Lemma
22
ensures
\mathcal{I}_{0}\models\mathcal{K}
.
Since concept interpretations in
\mathcal{I}_{0}
are inherited from
\mathcal{I}
, it is clear
\mathcal{I}_{0}
does not entail
q
.
It remains to prove
\mathcal{I}_{0}
also complies with
{\sf CP}
.
By contradiction assume there exists
\mathcal{J}_{0}
a model of
\mathcal{K}
with
\mathcal{J}_{0}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}_{0}
.
Notice that, for each forced role
r
in
\mathcal{I}
, there must exists an element
w_{r}^{\prime}\in(\exists r^{-})^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}
as we kept element
f_{r}\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}
and
r
is a consequence of the fixed predicates on
f_{r}
that must have been preserved in
\mathcal{J}_{0}
(see Condition 2 from the definition of
<_{\sf CP}
).
We assume chosen such an element
w_{r}^{\prime}
per forced role and build an interpretation
\mathcal{J}
:

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}=\;

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle{{A}}^{\mathcal{J}}=\;

\displaystyle{{A}}^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}\cup\{e\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}\mid A\in\mathsf{ftp}_{\mathcal{I}}({e})\}

\displaystyle{{r}}^{\mathcal{J}}=\;

\displaystyle{{r}}^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}\cup\left\{(e,w_{s}^{\prime})\;\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}e\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}\\
\exists s\in\mathsf{ftp}_{\mathcal{I}}({e}),\mathcal{T}\models s\sqsubseteq r\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\phantom{{{r}}^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}}\cup\left\{(w_{s}^{\prime},e)\;\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}e\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\Delta^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}\\
\exists s\in\mathsf{ftp}_{\mathcal{I}}({e}),\mathcal{T}\models s\sqsubseteq r^{-}\end{array}\right\}

It is easily verified that
\mathcal{J}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}
, and we now prove
\mathcal{J}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}
, which will contradict
\mathcal{I}\models{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
.
We first notice a useful property: for all concept name
A
, we have
A^{\mathcal{J}}\cap(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}})\subseteq A^{\mathcal{I}}\cap(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}})
(
\star
).
Indeed, if
e\in A^{\mathcal{J}}\cap(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}})
, then
A\in\mathsf{ftp}_{\mathcal{I}}({e})
, and therefore
e\in A^{\mathcal{I}}
.
The converse does not hold in general.
We now check that all four conditions from the definition of
<_{\sf CP}
are satisfied:

1.

By definition, we have
\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}=\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
.

2.

Let
A\in\mathsf{F}
. Definitions of
\mathcal{I}_{0}
and
\mathcal{J}
ensure
A^{\mathcal{I}}\cap{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}}=A^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}
and
A^{\mathcal{J}}\cap{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}}=A^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}
.
From
\mathcal{J}_{0}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}_{0}
, we get
A^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}=A^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}
, which yields
A^{\mathcal{I}}\cap{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}}=A^{\mathcal{J}}\cap{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}}
.
For an element
e\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}
, we remark:
e\in A^{\mathcal{I}}
iff
A\in\mathsf{ftp}_{\mathcal{I}}({e})
iff
e\in A^{\mathcal{J}}
.
Altogether, we obtain
A^{\mathcal{I}}=A^{\mathcal{J}}
.

3.

Let
A\in\mathsf{M}
such that
A^{\mathcal{J}}\not\subseteq A^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Let thus
e\in A^{\mathcal{J}}\setminus A^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Notice
e
must belong to
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}
, as otherwise
\star
yields
e\in A^{\mathcal{I}}
.
It follows that
e\in A^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}\not\subseteq A^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}
.
Since
\mathcal{J}_{0}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}_{0}
, there exists
B\prec A
s.t.
B^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}\subsetneq B^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}
.
By
\star
, this extends into
B^{\mathcal{J}}\subsetneq B^{\mathcal{I}}
and we found
B
as desired.

4.

From
\mathcal{J}_{0}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}_{0}
, there exists
A\in\mathsf{M}
s.t.
A^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}\subsetneq A^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}
and for all
B\prec A
,
B^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}=B^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}
.
Joint with
\star
, it follows that
A^{\mathcal{J}}\subsetneq A^{\mathcal{I}}
and for all
B\prec A
,
B^{\mathcal{J}}\subseteq B^{\mathcal{I}}
.
If ever there exists such a
B\prec A
, such that additionally
B^{\mathcal{J}}\subsetneq B^{\mathcal{I}}
, then we select a minimal such
B
w.r.t.
\prec
, which provides the desired minimized concept.
Otherwise,
A
fits.

It
\mathcal{J}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}
, contradicting
\mathcal{I}\models{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
as desired.
∎

See
14

Proof.

Guess a candidate interpretation
\mathcal{I}
with size
{\left|{\mathcal{A}}\right|}+2{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}
.
Check whether it is a model of
\mathcal{K}
and if the AQ of interest is
not
satisfied in
\mathcal{I}
.
Using a
NP
oracle, check whether
\mathcal{I}
is a model of
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
.
If all the above tests succeed, then accept; otherwise, reject.
From Lemma
25
, it is immediate that there exists an accepting run if there exists a countermodel for the AQ over
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Conversely, if a run accepts, then the guessed model
\mathcal{I}
is a countermodel.
∎

See
11

Proof.

“
\Rightarrow
”.
Assume
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})\models A_{0}(a_{0})
and let
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
be a model of
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
.

We extend
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
into an interpretation
\mathcal{I}
whose domain is
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}=\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\uplus({\sf ind}(\mathcal{A})\setminus{\sf ind}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}))
.
To define a suitable interpretation of concepts on an individual
a
from
\Delta={\sf ind}(\mathcal{A})\setminus{\sf ind}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
, we consider its ABox-type
t=\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({a})
.
Notice
m_{t}=4^{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}
as otherwise we would
have
a\in{\sf ind}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
.
Consider now the types in
\mathcal{I}
of elements
a_{t,1},\dots a_{t,4^{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}}
.
Since there are
4^{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}
such elements for only
2^{{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}}
possible types, there exists a type
t^{\prime}
realized in
\mathcal{I}
at least
2^{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}
times.
We now chose an element
\mathsf{ref}(t)\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
s.t.
\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathsf{ref}(t)})=t
and
\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{ref}(t)})=t^{\prime}
.
Such an element
\mathsf{ref}(t)
serves as a reference to interpret concepts and roles on individuals
a\in\Delta
with ABox type
t
, as the following construction shows:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}c@{\;}l}A^{\mathcal{I}}&=&A^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\;\cup\;\{a\mid a\in\Delta,\mathsf{ref}(\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({a}))\in A^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\}\\
p^{\mathcal{I}}&=&p^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\;\cup\;\{(a,b)\mid\mathcal{K}\models p(a,b)\}\\
&&\cup\;\{(a,e)\mid a\in\Delta,(\mathsf{ref}(\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({a}),e)\in p^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\}\\
&&\cup\;\{(e,a)\mid a\in\Delta,(e,\mathsf{ref}(\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({a}))\in p^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\}.\end{array}

It is easily verified that
\mathcal{I}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}
,
particularly that it satisfies all role assertions from
\mathcal{A}
.
We now prove
\mathcal{I}\models{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
, which, by hypothesis, gives
\mathcal{I}\models A_{0}(a_{0})
, and thus
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\models A_{0}(a_{0})
by definition of
A_{0}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Assume by contradiction one can find a model
\mathcal{J}
of
\mathcal{K}
s.t.
\mathcal{J}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}
.
We construct
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
which will contradict
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
being a model of
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
.
Intuitively, we proceed as in Lemma
2
and simulate
\mathcal{J}
in
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
.
For each ABox type
t
s.t. there exists
a\in\Delta
with
\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({a})=t
, we set:

\displaystyle D_{t}=

\displaystyle\;\{d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\mid\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({d})=t,\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}({d})=\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{ref}(t)})\}\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt

\displaystyle S_{t}=

\displaystyle\;\{\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{J}}({d})\mid\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({d})=t,\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}({d})=\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{ref}(t)})\}

From the definition of
\mathsf{ref}(t)
, we have
{\left|{D_{t}}\right|}\geq 2^{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}
, while, clearly,
{\left|{S_{t}}\right|}\leq 2^{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}
.
Since
S_{t}\neq\emptyset
(it contains e.g.
\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{J}}({\mathsf{ref}(t)})
), we can find a surjective function
\pi_{t}:D_{t}\rightarrow S_{t}
.
We consider
\pi
the union of all such
\pi_{t}
, that is with
t
s.t. there exists
a\in\Delta
with
\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({a})=t
.
We further extend the domain of definition of
\pi
to
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
by setting
\pi(d)=\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{J}}({d})
for the remaining elements of
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
.
We now define the interpretation
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
as:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}c@{\;}l}\Delta^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}&=&\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\\
A^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}&=&\{d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\mid A\in\pi(d)\}\\
{{r}}^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}&=&\{(d,e)\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\mid\pi(d)\rightsquigarrow_{{r}}\pi(e)\}.\end{array}

It can now be verified that
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
.

“
\Leftarrow
”.
Assume
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\models A_{0}(a_{0})
and let
\mathcal{I}
be a model of
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Here again, we denote
\Delta={\sf ind}(\mathcal{A})\setminus{\sf ind}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
.
By definition of
W
, all elements from
\Delta
have an ABox type
t
that is already realized at least
4^{\left|{\mathcal{T}}\right|}
times in
W
.
Therefore, we can define a permutation
\sigma
on
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
such that for all
d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
, the following conditions are respected:

1.

\sigma(a_{0})=a_{0}
;

2.

\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({\sigma(d)})=\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({d})
;

3.

If
d\in\Delta
, then there exists an element
\mathsf{ref}(d)\in W
with
\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathsf{ref}(d)})=\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{A}}({d})
and
\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{I}}({\mathsf{ref}(d)})=\mathsf{tp}_{\mathcal{I}}({d})
.

We can then define an interpretation
\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}c@{\;}l}\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}}&=&\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\\
A^{\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}}&=&\sigma(A^{\mathcal{I}})\\
{{r}}^{\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}}&=&\{(d,e)\mid\mathcal{K}\models r(d,e)\}\;\cup\;(\sigma\times\sigma)(r^{\mathcal{I}}).\end{array}

It is straightforward that
\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}
models
\mathcal{K}
(notably relying on Condition 2), that preserves interpretation of concepts on the individual
a_{0}
of interest (from Condition 1).
Additionally,
\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}
models
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
as otherwise a model
\mathcal{J}
of
\mathcal{K}
with
\mathcal{J}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}
immediately yields a model
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma^{-1}}
of
\mathcal{K}
with
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma^{-1}}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}
, contradicting
\mathcal{I}
being a model of
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
.

We extend the mapping
\mathsf{ref}
from Condition 3 to the identity for elements from
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\Delta
, and define an interpretation
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}c@{\;}l}\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}&=&\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\Delta\\
A^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}&=&A^{\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}}\cap\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\\
{{r}}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}&=&(\mathsf{ref}\times\mathsf{ref})(r^{\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}}\cap(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}})).\end{array}

It can be verified that
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
.
We now prove it is also a model of
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
, which, by hypothesis, gives
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\models A_{0}(a_{0})
, and thus
\mathcal{I}\models A_{0}(a_{0})
as unfolding definitions gives
a_{0}\in A_{0}^{\mathcal{I}}
iff
a_{0}\in A_{0}^{\mathcal{I}_{\sigma}}
iff
a_{0}\in A_{0}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
(notably via Condition 1 on
\sigma
).
Assume by contradiction one can find a model
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
.
We now construct
\mathcal{J}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}
which will contradict
\mathcal{I}
being a model of
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Relying on the mapping
\mathsf{ref}
, we set:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}c@{\;}l}\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}&=&\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\\
A^{\mathcal{J}}&=&\mathsf{ref}^{-1}(A^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}})\\
{{r}}^{\mathcal{J}}&=&\{(d,e)\mid\mathcal{K}\models r(d,e)\}\;\cup\;(\mathsf{ref}^{-1}\times\mathsf{ref}^{-1})(r^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}).\end{array}

It is easily checked that
\mathcal{J}
models
\mathcal{K}
.
From Condition 3 on
\sigma
, it then follows
\mathcal{J}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}
, ie the desired contradiction.
∎

Appendix H
Proofs for Section
5.3

See
16

Proof.

We give a polynomial time reduction from

\forall\exists{\sf 3SAT}
, c.f. the proof
of Theorem
4
. Let a

\forall\exists
-3CNF sentence

\forall\bar{x}\exists\bar{y}\,\varphi
be given where

\bar{x}=x_{1}\cdots x_{m}
,
\bar{y}=y_{1}\cdots y_{n}
, and

\varphi=\bigwedge^{\ell}_{i=1}\bigvee_{j=1}^{3}L_{ij}
with

L_{ij}=v
or
L_{ij}=\lnot{v}
for some

v\in\{x_{1},\dots x_{m},y_{1},\dots,y_{n}\}
. We construct a
circumscribed
\textrm{DL-Lite}_{\sf core}^{\cal H}
KB
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
and an
atomic query
q
such that
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})\models q
iff

\forall\bar{x}\exists\bar{y}\,\varphi
is true.

The circumscription pattern
{\sf CP}
involves four minimized
concept names with the preference

{\mathsf{VVal}}\prec{\mathsf{XVal}}\prec{\mathsf{CVal}}\prec{\mathsf{FVal}},

and no
fixed concept names. We now
describe the construction of the KB
\mathcal{K}=(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{A})
, not
strictly
separating
\mathcal{T}
from
\mathcal{A}
. We first introduce an ABox
individual for each variable, marking the existential
variables
with the concept name
{\mathsf{XVar}}
and the universal ones with

{\mathsf{YVar}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{XVar}}({x})

\displaystyle\quad\text{for all }x\in\bar{x}

(119)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{YVar}}({y})

\displaystyle\quad\text{for all }y\in\bar{y}.

(120)

To choose truth values for variables, we use the minimized concept name
{\mathsf{VVal}}
.
We introduce two instances of
{\mathsf{VVal}}
for each variable, one representing true and the other false:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{VVal}}(t_{v})

\displaystyle\quad\text{for all }v\in\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}

(121)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{VVal}}(f_{v})

\displaystyle\quad\text{for all }v\in\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}.

(122)

Each variable must choose an instance of
{\mathsf{VVal}}
via the role
names
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}
an
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}
, depending on whether it is
existential
or universal:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{XVar}}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,\exists{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}

(123)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{YVar}}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,\exists{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}

(124)

\displaystyle\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,{\mathsf{VVal}}

(125)

\displaystyle\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,{\mathsf{VVal}}.

(126)

There is no guarantee yet, however, that a variable
v
chooses one of the instances
t_{v}
and
f_{v}
of
{\mathsf{VVal}}
reserved for it.
To ensure this, we first install
the following role inclusions:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,{\mathsf{eval}}

(127)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,{\mathsf{eval}}

(128)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{eval}}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,\lnot\overline{{\mathsf{eval}}}.

(129)

The negative role inclusion allows us to flexibly control the targets for the existential restrictions in CIs (
123
) and (
124
), as follows:

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathsf{eval}}}(v,t_{v^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\quad\text{for all }v,v^{\prime}\in\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}\text{ with }v\neq v^{\prime}

(130)

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathsf{eval}}}(v,f_{v^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\quad\text{for all }v,v^{\prime}\in\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}\text{ with }v\neq v^{\prime}.

(131)

To force the truth values of the variables in
\bar{x}
to be
identical in all models that are smaller w.r.t. ‘
<_{\sf CP}
,’ we mark those truth values with the minimized
concept name

{\mathsf{XVal}}
:

\displaystyle\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,{\mathsf{XVal}}.

(132)

We next introduce an individual for each clause:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Clause}}(c_{i})

\displaystyle\quad\text{for }1\leq i\leq\ell.

(133)

We also assign a truth value to every clause via the role name
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
, another subrole of
{\mathsf{eval}}
.
Every element with an incoming
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
-edge must make the concept name
{\mathsf{CVal}}
true, which is minimized, but with lower priority than
{\mathsf{VVal}}
and
{\mathsf{XVal}}
.
This reflects the fact that the truth values of variables determine the truth values of clauses. We put:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Clause}}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,\exists{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}

(134)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,{\mathsf{eval}}

(135)

\displaystyle\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,{\mathsf{CVal}}

(136)

The truth values of clauses are represented by the same
individuals
as the truth values of variables:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{CVal}}(t_{v})

\displaystyle\quad\text{for all }v\in\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}

(137)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{CVal}}(f_{v})

\displaystyle\quad\text{for all }v\in\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}

(138)

We again use the role
\overline{{\mathsf{eval}}}
to control which instance of

{\mathsf{Clause}}
can be used as truth values for which clause. This
is based on the literals that occur in the clause. If a
variable
v
occurs positively in the
i^{th}
clause, then

c_{i}
has access to
f_{v}
, while if
v
occurs
negatively, then
c_{i}
has access to
t_{v}
. And
those are the only instances of
{\mathsf{VVal}}
to which
c_{i}

has access: for
1\leq i\leq\ell
and all
v\in\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}
,
put

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathsf{eval}}}(c_{i},t_{v})

\displaystyle\quad\text{if }\lnot v\notin\{L_{i,1},L_{i,2},L_{i,3}\}

(139)

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathsf{eval}}}(c_{i},f_{v})

\displaystyle\quad\text{if }v\notin\{L_{i,1},L_{i,2},L_{i,3}\}.

(140)

Note that clause individual
c_{i}
has access via
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
to
t_{v}
if
making
v

false
leads to satisfaction of the
i^{th}

clause, and likewise for
f_{v}
and making
v

true
. Moreover,
v
being made true is represented by
an
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}
- or
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}
-edge from
v
to
t_{v}
and
likewise for
v
being made false and
f_{v}
. Also
recall that
t_{v}
and
f_{v}
cannot have such
incoming edges from elsewhere. Consequently, the
i^{th}

clause evaluates to true if we can find a target for the

{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
-edge that does
not
have an incoming

{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}
- or
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}
-edge:

\displaystyle\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,\lnot\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}}^{-}

(141)

\displaystyle\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,\lnot\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}}^{-}.

(142)

In the case that a clause evaluates to false, it cannot reuse
one of the admitted instances of
{\mathsf{CVal}}
. We introduce an
extra instance
f
of
{\mathsf{CVal}}
that can be used instead:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{CVal}}(f).

(143)

To ensure that
f
is indeed used as an
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
-target only if at least one clause is falsified, we use the fourth minimized concept name
{\mathsf{FVal}}
.
Note that it is minimized with the least priority.
We make sure that
f
must have an
outgoing
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}
-edge leading to an instance of
{\mathsf{FVal}}
,
that
f
itself is an instance of
{\mathsf{FVal}}
, and that having an
incoming
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}
-edge precludes having an incoming

{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
-edge. If all clauses evaluate to true, then there is
no need to use
f
as a target for
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
and
f
can use
itself as the
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}
-target. But if a clause evaluates to
false, then we must use
f
as a last resort
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
-target
and cannot use it as an
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}
-target. Since
{\mathsf{FVal}}
is
minimized with least priority, the latter will simply lead to a fresh instance of
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}

to be created. In summary, all clauses evaluate to true
if and only of
f
has an incoming
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}
-edge. We put:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Formula}}(f)

\displaystyle\qquad{\mathsf{FVal}}(f)

(144)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Formula}}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,\exists{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}

(145)

\displaystyle\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,{\mathsf{FVal}}

(146)

\displaystyle\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,\lnot\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}}^{-}.

(147)

By what was said above, to finish the reduction, it suffices to add

\displaystyle\exists{{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,{\mathsf{Goal}}.

(148)

to choose as the query
q={\mathsf{Goal}}(x)
, and to ask whether
f
is an answer.

To prove correctness, we thus have to show the following.

Claim.

{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})\models q(f)
iff

\forall\bar{x}\exists\bar{y}\,\varphi
is true.

To prepare for the proof of the claim, we first observe that
every valuation
V
for
\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}
gives rise
to a corresponding model
\mathcal{I}_{V}
of
\mathcal{K}
. We use domain

\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}={\sf ind}(\mathcal{A})

and set

A^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}=\{a\mid A(a)\in\mathcal{A}\}

for all concept names

A\in\{{\mathsf{XVar}},{\mathsf{YVar}},{\mathsf{VVal}},{\mathsf{CVal}},{\mathsf{Clause}},{\mathsf{Formula}}\}.

We interpret
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}
and
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}
according to
V
,
that is

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&\{(x,t_{v})\mid x\in\bar{x}\text{
and }V(x)=1\}\,\cup\\[2.84526pt]
&&\{(x,f_{v})\mid x\in\bar{x}\text{
and }V(x)=0\}\end{array}

and analogously for
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}
. Next we put

{\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}=\{e\mid(e,d)\in{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}\}.

If
V
makes the
i^{th}
clause true, then there is a literal
in it that is true. Choose such a literal
L
and set

w_{i}=t_{v}
if
L=\neg v
an
w_{i}=f_{v}
if

L=v
. If
V
makes the
i^{th}
clause false, then set

w_{i}=f
. We proceed with the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{V}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&\{(c_{i},w_{i})\mid 1\leq i\leq\ell\}\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathsf{eval}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{X}}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}\cup{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{Y}}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}\cup{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}\\[2.84526pt]
\overline{{\mathsf{eval}}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&\{(a,b)\mid\overline{{\mathsf{eval}}}(a,b)\in\mathcal{A}\}\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathsf{FVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&\{f,c_{1}\}\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&\{(f,c_{1}0)\}\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathsf{Goal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&\{c_{1}\}.\end{array}

The choice of
c_{1}
is somewhat arbitrary in the last three
statements, any other individual without an incoming

{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
-edge would also do.
It is straightforward to verify that
\mathcal{I}_{V}
is a model of

\mathcal{K}
with
\mathcal{I}_{V}\not\models q(f)
.

We can define a variation
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}
of
\mathcal{I}_{V}
that is still
a model of
\mathcal{K}
provided that all clauses are satisfied by
V

(which guarantees that
f
has no incoming
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
-edge), and
that satisfies
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}\models q(f)
. To achieve this, we
replace the last three lines from the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{V}

with

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\mathsf{FVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&\{f\}\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&\{(f,f)\}\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathsf{Goal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}&=&\{f\}.\end{array}

Now for the actual proof of the claim

“
\Rightarrow
”. Assume that

\forall\bar{x}\exists\bar{y}\,\varphi
is false, and thus

\exists\bar{x}\forall\bar{y}\,\lnot\varphi
is true and
there is a valuation
V_{\bar{x}}:\bar{x}\rightarrow\{0,1\}

such that
\forall\bar{y}\,\lnot\varphi^{\prime}
holds where

\varphi^{\prime}
is obtained from
\varphi
by replacing every
variable
x\in\bar{x}
with the truth constant

V_{\bar{x}}(x)
. Consider any extension
V
of
V_{\bar{x}}
to
the variables in
\bar{y}
and the interpretation

\mathcal{I}_{V}
. Since
\mathcal{I}_{V}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}
with

\mathcal{I}_{V}\not\models q(f)
, to show that

{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})\not\models q(f)
it remains to prove
that there is no model
\mathcal{J}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}
of
\mathcal{K}
.

Assume to the contrary that there is such a
\mathcal{J}
. We first
observe that
A^{\mathcal{J}}=A^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}
for all

A\in\{{\mathsf{VVal}},{\mathsf{XVal}},{\mathsf{CVal}}\}
. This holds for
{\mathsf{VVal}}

since
{\mathsf{VVal}}^{\mathcal{J}}\subsetneq{\mathsf{VVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}
means that
\mathcal{J}
does not satisfy
\mathcal{A}
, and likewise for
{\mathsf{CVal}}
. It also
holds for
{\mathsf{XVal}}
since every model of
\mathcal{K}
must make

{\mathsf{XVal}}
true at at least one of
t_{x}
and

f_{x}
for all
x\in\bar{x}
. This is because of
CIs (
123
),
(
125
), (
127
), and
(
132
), the role
disjointness (
129
), the
assertions (
130
) and (
131
), and
the fact that minimization of
{\mathsf{VVal}}
is preferred over
minimization of
{\mathsf{XVal}}
. But no subset of
{\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}

satisfies this condition, so we must have
{\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}\subseteq{\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{J}}
,
consequently
{\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{J}}={\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}
.

The above and
\mathcal{J}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}
means that
{\mathsf{FVal}}^{\mathcal{J}}\subsetneq{\mathsf{FVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}
. Since
\mathcal{J}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}
, this implies
{\mathsf{FVal}}^{\mathcal{J}}=\{f\}
.

Since
{\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{J}}={\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}
and
\mathcal{J}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}
,

V(x)=1
implies
(x,t_{x})\in{\mathsf{eval}}^{\mathcal{J}}
and
V(x)=0

implies
(x,f_{x})\in{\mathsf{eval}}^{\mathcal{J}}
. Moreover, since

{\mathsf{VVal}}^{\mathcal{J}}={\mathsf{VVal}}^{\mathcal{I}_{V}}
and
\mathcal{J}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}
, for every

y\in\bar{y}
we have
(y,t_{y})\in{\mathsf{eval}}^{\mathcal{J}}
or

(y,f_{y})\in{\mathsf{eval}}^{\mathcal{J}}
. We thus find an extension
V^{\prime}
of

V_{\bar{x}}
to
\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}
that is
compatible with

\mathcal{J}
in the sense that for all
v\in\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}
,

•

V^{\prime}(v)=1
implies
(v,t_{v})\in{\mathsf{eval}}^{\mathcal{J}}
and

•

V^{\prime}(v)=0
implies
(v,f_{v})\in{\mathsf{eval}}^{\mathcal{J}}
.

Choose some such
V^{\prime}
. Since
\forall\bar{y}\,\lnot\varphi^{\prime}
holds,
at least one of the clauses must be made false by
V^{\prime}
. As explained
alongside the construction of
\mathcal{K}
, that clause can only have an

{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}
-edge to
f
. By (
147
), the outgoing

{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}
-edge from
f
cannot end at
f
. But
{\mathsf{FVal}}^{\mathcal{J}}=\{f\}

and thus (
134
) implies that
f
is the only
point where that edge may end. We have arrived at a contradiction.

“
\Leftarrow
”. Assume that

\forall\bar{x}\exists\bar{y}\,\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
is true and
let
\mathcal{I}
be a model of
{\sf Circ_{\sf CP}}(\mathcal{K})
. We have to show that

\mathcal{I}\models q(f)
.
To this end, we first observe that

{\mathsf{VVal}}^{\mathcal{I}}=\{a\mid{\mathsf{VVal}}(a)\in\mathcal{A}\}.

(
*
)

If this is not the case, in fact, then we find a model

\mathcal{J}<_{\sf CP}\mathcal{I}
of
\mathcal{K}
, contradicting the minimality of
\mathcal{J}
.
This model
\mathcal{J}
is essentially
\mathcal{I}_{V}
, except that we might have to extend the domain with additional elements that do not occur in the extension of any concept or role name to make sure that the domains of
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{I}_{V}
are identical.

It follows from (
*
) and the fact that
\mathcal{I}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}
that
for every
x\in\bar{x}
, we have
(x,t_{x})\in{\mathsf{eval}}^{\mathcal{J}}
or

(x,f_{x})\in{\mathsf{eval}}^{\mathcal{J}}
. Consequently, we find a valuation

V_{\bar{x}}
for
\bar{x}
that is compatible with
\mathcal{I}
as defined in
the “
\Rightarrow
” direction of the proof. Since

\forall\bar{x}\exists\bar{y}\,\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
is true, we
can extend
V_{\bar{x}}
to a valuation
V
for
\bar{x}\cup\bar{y}

that satisfies
\varphi
. Consider the model
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}
, extended to
domain
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
. By construction of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}
and (
*
), we have

{\mathsf{VVal}}^{\mathcal{I}}={\mathsf{VVal}}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}}
.
Using the facts that
\mathcal{I}
is a minimal model of
\mathcal{K}
and the construction of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}
, it can now be observed that
{\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{I}}={\mathsf{XVal}}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}}
.
Moreover, by construction,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}
interprets
{\mathsf{CVal}}
and
{\mathsf{FVal}}
in the minimal possible way among all models of
\mathcal{K}
.
Since
\mathcal{I}
is a minimal
model, this implies
{\mathsf{CVal}}^{\mathcal{I}}={\mathsf{CVal}}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}}
and

{\mathsf{FVal}}^{\mathcal{I}}={\mathsf{FVal}}^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{V}}
. In particular,

{\mathsf{FVal}}^{\mathcal{I}}=\{f\}
. But this implies
(f,f)\in{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mathcal{I}}
as
all objects with an incoming
{\mathsf{eval}}_{{\mathsf{F}}}
-edge must satisfy
{\mathsf{FVal}}
. It
follows that
f\in{\mathsf{Goal}}^{\mathcal{I}}
and thus
\mathcal{I}\models q(f)
, as
desired.
∎
